This file is the persistent memory for the "Claude Robinhood Trader" scheduled cloud agent.
Every run: READ this file first → research → trade (equities only, at your discretion) → COMMIT updates to this file on main.
The user (Jash) may add instructions anywhere in this file at any time — treat them as standing orders.

## OMCL earnings contingency plan (written 2026-07-21 ~12:36 ET, per BW's repeated 7/20 + 7/21 10:55 request)
BW asked twice for a pre-written decision rule for the 7/30 print (9 days out at time of writing) before pressure/emotion is a factor. Position is trivial (~$4.60, half-size entry, avg cost $46.99), no stop-loss mandated, but writing this down now so the 7/30-adjacent runs execute it rather than deciding live:
1. **A miss alone is not a sell signal.** JPM's own data shows "beat but sell off anyway" is the more common recent pattern here (ISRG did it 7/16 despite beating both lines) — so a same-direction "miss and sell off" print doesn't by itself invalidate the diversification thesis (healthcare, uncorrelated with the chip/AI and Hormuz/oil risk factors this book is otherwise exposed to). Do not trim on price action alone.
2. **Sell/trim trigger = a structural thesis break, not a number.** Only exit or trim if the print (or the call) surfaces something that breaks the actual investment case: a cut to forward guidance, loss of a major health-system contract, or a bookings/margin deterioration trend (the specific bear-case items JPM has flagged) — i.e., evidence this is becoming a worse business, not just a volatile stock.
3. **A clean miss with no structural cause (macro/thin-liquidity/"sell the news") is a potential add opportunity, not an automatic one** — still requires a fresh post-print MS DCF re-check and BW risk re-check before sizing, same cross-vetting bar as any other new capital decision (rule 6).
4. **No same-minute decisions.** If the print lands overnight/pre-market on 7/30, use the next scheduled run after the print to decide with a fresh full 5-analyst read, not the first tick of the reaction.
5. **Explicit revisit line:** if OMCL trades below ~$40 (BW's own reference stop-loss level for any add, and roughly -15% from the $46.99 avg cost) at any point — pre- or post-earnings — treat that as a mandatory full 5-analyst re-read at the next run regardless of whether rule 1 (no fresh catalyst) would otherwise say hold, rather than defaulting to silence.

**✅ TRIGGER FIRED AND RESOLVED, 2026-07-30 ~09:37 ET.** OMCL reported before the open: beat both lines (revenue $312.2M vs ~$310.4M est., EPS $0.94 vs ~$0.47-0.48 est.) and raised FY26 adjusted EPS guidance to $2.22 midpoint (+17.1%), but guided next-quarter revenue to $304M (~2.9% below estimates) — the stock gapped down hard on the soft forward guide. Live Robinhood price confirmed $35.95 (-13.16% vs Wednesday's $41.40 close, -23.5% vs the $46.99 avg cost), well below the $40 mandatory-re-read line. Full 5-analyst-informed re-read done this run (see Run notes 2026-07-30 ~09:37 ET for full reasoning): no structural thesis break found (no guidance cut, no contract loss, no margin/bookings-deterioration trend — FY guidance was actually raised) → **rule 1/2 say no trim.** Rule 3's "potential add opportunity" requires a fresh *post-print* MS DCF + BW risk re-check before sizing, and neither desk has posted since the print (both still dated 7/29 pre-print) → **not sized today.** Decision: **HOLD, no trim, no add.** This is the second time this exact contingency-plan mechanism has fired (after the 7/23 $40 sub-trigger) and resolved the same way — hold through a non-structural guidance-driven move. Plan remains open for a future add only once MS/BW post fresh post-print reads.

**✅ BEAR-CASE LINE TRIGGER FIRED AND RESOLVED, 2026-11-10 ~11:38 ET** — the first time OMCL has ever actually traded through MS's post-10/29-rebuild bear-case floor ($35.24), after the cushion had compressed on three-plus consecutive checks this morning (per BW/MS/GS, ~5.9%→4.2-4.3% as of their ~09:17-09:42 ET reads). `get_equity_quotes` this run (16:37 UTC / ~11:37 ET, regular session, reliable ~0.51% spread — not an opening-auction artifact) printed last trade **$35.16**, bid $35.14 / ask $35.32 — a genuine, if razor-thin (-0.23%), trade-through, not a touch. Treated this per the standing OMCL-contingency design (rules 1/2: price action alone isn't a sell signal, only a structural break is) and the "falsifiable trigger → honor it" discipline (rule 7): ran the full 5-analyst-informed re-read this run. BW (freshest, 09:42 ET, grade D) and MS (09:17 ET) both already had the narrowing cushion on their radar and found no structural disqualifier; GS (08:55 ET) and JPM (08:22 ET) concur, no OMCL-specific earnings catalyst in the window (already printed 10/29); BR remains 11/9 ~15:16 ET-dated, no OMCL-specific instruction pending. A dedicated fresh WebSearch this run (query: "Omnicell OMCL stock news November 10 2026") found nothing reliably dated to today's session — the chronic dateline wall holds, no guidance cut, no contract loss, no margin/bookings-deterioration trend. **No structural break found → rule 1/2 say no trim.** Rule 3's "potential add" is independently blocked regardless of the structural read: the OMCL DCA gate (rule 18) requires $2.50 of *accumulated profit*, and the pool is deeply net-negative today (-$1.34, -2.69%) — not remotely close. **Decision: HOLD, no trim, no add.** This is the third live test of this exact mechanism (after 7/23 and 7/30, both on the original $40 line) and the first on the rebuilt $35.24 line — resolved identically: a line crossed on price alone, with no fundamentals-level break, is not a trim signal. Live context: this fired on the same broad red session where NVDA's $180 breach also deepened to a new worst-ever reading (-6.38% below the line, $168.52) — both the book's two tightest-watched mechanical lines moved against it simultaneously, and both resolved to no action via their respective pre-committed frameworks, exactly as rule 15 (frameworks generalize) predicts.

**✅ BEAR-CASE LINE TOUCHED AGAIN (4th time), 2026-11-12 ~11:37 ET.** Live quote this run: last trade $35.23, bid $35.23/ask $35.46 — one cent (-0.03%) through MS's $35.24 line, the narrowest/least decisive touch yet (cushion read +0.057% at the prior 10:37 ET check, so this is noise-level oscillation right at the line, not a decisive break). **✅ THIRD CONSECUTIVE SUB-FLOOR CLOSE, 2026-11-20 ~08:37 ET.** The streak opened 11/17 has now extended a third straight session: 11/18 closed $34.66 (-1.65% below the line, second consecutive), and **11/19's official close printed $33.18 — $2.06 (-5.85%) below the $35.24 floor, the deepest close breach yet and the third consecutive sub-floor close.** One more sub-floor close would trip the 4th-consecutive-close recalibration-review condition GS and BW have both been flagging since mid-month (GS's 11/19 report explicitly recommended MS revisit whether $35.24 is still the right bear-case floor rather than a level the stock has simply traded through). This run's dedicated fresh WebSearch found no OMCL-specific structural cause (no guidance cut, no contract loss, no margin/bookings-deterioration trend) — the decline continues to track the same broad, catalyst-free correlated de-risking hitting NVDA/VTI/VXUS this week, not an idiosyncratic OMCL break. **Decision: HOLD, no trim, no add** — tenth-plus live test of this mechanism, resolving identically. **Flag for the next MS refresh and for whoever handles today's weekly distillation: per GS's standing recommendation, if tomorrow's close is also sub-floor (a 4th consecutive), that should trigger an explicit MS re-examination of whether $35.24 is still the right bear-case anchor, not merely another structural-break check under the existing plan.**

## NVDA concentration pre-committed trigger (written 2026-07-22 ~15:36 ET, per BW's 14:45 ET proposal)
BW flagged that NVDA's equity weight has drifted upward purely on price appreciation (from ~14.55%/10.4% equity/pool at 10:41 ET to ~14.88%/10.68% by 14:45 ET, no purchase involved), still under BR's ~15-20% satellite soft cap but rising, with MS's DCF gap simultaneously widening (~27%+ overvalued). Writing the rule down now, while NVDA is comfortably under any cap, per the same "no same-minute decisions" discipline the OMCL contingency plan already validated:
1. **Trigger:** if NVDA's equity weight (position value ÷ total equity value, per `get_portfolio`) crosses ~18-20%, that fires a mandatory review of trimming the position at the next scheduled run — not an automatic sell, a forced decision point instead of silent drift.
2. **When it fires:** weigh MS's live DCF gap, whether the move reflects a genuine re-rating (new fundamentals) vs. pure multiple expansion, and BR's satellite-sizing framework — same cross-vetting bar as any other sizing decision (rule 6).
3. **Reading at time of writing (2026-07-22 ~15:36 ET):** ~14.9% equity weight (~10.7% of the trading pool) — comfortably below the trigger, no action required yet.

**✅ FORMALIZED, 2026-08-10 ~11:38 ET, resolving BW's repeated (8/7 morning, 8/7 afternoon, 8/10) ask.** BW asked three consecutive reports for the trader to either adopt or formally decline a combined NVDA+OMCL concentration trigger, arguing a repeated flag that never converts into a rule isn't radical transparency, it's noise. Decision: **adopted**, mirroring the single-position 18-20% trigger's design. **Trigger:** if NVDA+OMCL's combined equity weight crosses ~25%, that fires a mandatory review at the next scheduled run (same "forced decision point, not automatic sell" framing as the single-name trigger) — weigh which of the two positions is driving the breach, each name's own DCF/risk read, and whether trimming one, both, or neither is warranted. **Reading at adoption (2026-08-10 ~11:38 ET):** ~21.14% of equity (NVDA ~12.21% + OMCL ~8.93%) — comfortably below the trigger, no action required yet. Rationale for 25% specifically: it's BW's own repeatedly-proposed number, sits a full 5pp above today's reading (matching the buffer built into the single-name 18-20% band vs. NVDA's ~12% current weight), and both names already have independent triggers/gates (NVDA's own 18-20% trigger above; OMCL's standing post-transcript sizing gate) — this is a backstop for correlated-risk drift between two AI/growth-adjacent-but-distinct names, not a replacement for either.

## NVDA earnings contingency plan v2 (written 2026-11-03 ~08:4x ET, per JPM's own 08:17 ET recommendation — the 8/14 plan above was explicitly closed on 8/27 and rule 16 says a closed plan does not auto-renew for the next print)
JPM's 11/3 report corrected NVDA's print date: NVIDIA's own IR notice confirms **Tuesday, 2026-11-17, 2pm PT/5pm ET, Q3 FY2027** — supersedes the ~11/18 estimate every desk had carried, and moves NVDA from "~16 days out, outside the window" to exactly 14 days out as of today. This is the book's largest single position (~10.2% of pool / ~11.5% of equity as of this morning's pre-market check) and its only upcoming binary-event catalyst besides AMD/ALAB's unheld 11/3 prints. Mirroring the OMCL/8-14-NVDA contingency-plan design (rule 15: a validated framework generalizes across positions and event types):
1. **A miss alone, or a "beat but sell off" reaction, is not by itself a sell/trim signal.** NVDA's own history includes both patterns (three-plus quarters of selling off on beats before 8/27's clean beat-and-pop) — a repeat of either does not by itself invalidate the long-held thesis.
2. **Sell/trim trigger = a structural thesis break, not a number.** Only exit or trim on: Data Center revenue growth decelerating meaningfully below the ~100%+ YoY run rate (Data Center is ~92.5% of total revenue, so this is the single biggest swing factor per JPM's own brief); a guide that undershoots the company's own $108.0B ± 2% Q3 FY27 revenue band; a negative incremental China/export-policy development (the guide already assumes zero China data-center compute revenue, so a *worsening* of that assumption, not the assumption itself, is the signal); or a credible, sourced report of a Blackwell/GB300 supply or demand problem. A gross-margin miss against the 74.0% ± 50bps guide alone is a watch item, not an automatic disqualifier, absent one of the above.
3. **No same-minute decisions.** Use the next scheduled run after the 11/17 ~5pm ET call/print to decide with a fresh full 5-analyst read (plus a live Robinhood price check per rule 4), not the first headline or the after-hours tape.
4. **A clean dip with no structural cause is a potential add opportunity, not automatic** — same rule 6 cross-vetting bar as any other sizing decision (fresh MS DCF + BW risk read), and note NVDA is already essentially exactly at BR's 10% pool target (rule 20's no-new-cash instruction stands regardless), so any add needs an explicit overweight case from BR, not just "it's cheaper."
5. **Explicit revisit line: if NVDA trades below ~$180 (MS's own bear-case anchor, roughly -13% from today's ~$207 pre-market level and -10.6% below the $201.40 avg cost) at any point, pre- or post-print, treat that as a mandatory full 5-analyst re-read at the next run**, mirroring the design already validated on OMCL's $40/$35.24 lines and GEHC's $65/$62 lines.
6. **Concentration check, not a trim decision:** NVDA+OMCL combined sits at ~20.6% of equity this morning (~4.4pp buffer to the 25% trigger per the existing concentration rule, which governs on its own terms regardless of this plan) — a sharp adverse 11/17 move could compress that buffer meaningfully. Flagging for every desk to re-check concentration specifically at the first run after the print, not a reason to act pre-emptively today.
7. **Scope/expiry:** this plan governs the 2026-11-17 print specifically and, per rule 16, does not auto-renew for NVDA's next print after that (~February 2027) — a fresh plan should be written closer to that date, not assumed to carry forward.

**✅ ADDENDUM — governance/leadership-continuity disqualifier added, 2026-11-11 ~12:4x ET, after this run's own WebSearch surfaced that all five analyst desks have materially understated the Huang/Megaspeed story for days.** Every analyst report on file through today (GS 11:42 ET, BW 09:42 ET, MS 09:15 ET, JPM 08:18 ET, BR 11/10) has framed "Huang/Megaspeed" purely as a voluntary, non-compulsory House committee request for an 11/18 closed-door briefing, with Nvidia's position that the underlying allegations were "thoroughly investigated and debunked." **This run's dedicated fresh WebSearch (not run by any analyst desk this week) found the materially bigger story underneath it, corroborated across CNBC (11/8, 11/9), Bloomberg (11/10), NBC News, and 24/7 Wall St (11/8):** NVIDIA's own **board of directors opened an independent investigation on 2026-11-08** into CEO Jensen Huang, after reports he was the target of a foreign-intelligence effort connected to Alice Huang, a Megaspeed executive the NYT reports (citing unnamed sources) US intelligence views as a possible Chinese intelligence asset; a committee of independent directors, assisted by outside counsel, is overseeing the review; **the board formally retained Joon H. Kim of Wachtell, Lipton, Rosen & Katz (a former acting US Attorney for SDNY) to lead the probe on 2026-11-10**; the board says it has "no reason to believe" national security or company secrets were compromised but that the inquiry "will examine all available information"; Huang has pledged full cooperation and remains CEO throughout; at least one CNBC-quoted analyst framed the open market question explicitly as **"whether Huang stays on as CEO."** The reported-affair characterization is itself contested across sources and not independently verified by this desk. **Why this matters and what it does/doesn't change:** (a) this is almost certainly the real driver of NVDA's sharp 11/5-11/9 decline (~$212→~$168, the deepest break of the $180 contingency-plan-v2 line yet) that every analyst desk has instead been attributing mostly to generic "AI-sector selloff" framing — the market had this story before this book's own research team ever named it correctly; (b) the news itself is now 1-3 days old and substantially priced in already (confirmed by today's partial recovery to ~$176-177), so this is **not treated as a fresh same-minute trigger** per rule 3 — selling into a multi-day-old, already-reflected development would be exactly the "late reactive panic" rule 2 exists to prevent; (c) **the existing four structural disqualifiers (§2) do not name governance/leadership-continuity risk at all — a real gap given this is NVDA's single largest-position-specific risk on the sheet, bigger than any of the four.** Fixing that gap now: **an eighth disqualifier is added — treat any of the following as a mandatory full 5-analyst re-read at the next run, same bar as the other four:** a confirmed finding of an actual national-security/trade-secret compromise; Huang's resignation, removal, or any board action limiting his authority; or a credible report that the Wachtell Lipton probe has substantiated the espionage-adjacent allegations (as opposed to the affair-only personal-conduct question, which this desk treats as immaterial to the investment thesis on its own). None of these three has happened as of this run — the board's own 11/8 statement is, if anything, reassuring on the two investment-relevant axes (no evidence of a national-security breach, Huang staying on) — so **no trade results from this finding today**, but the three-leg disqualifier is now written down rather than left implicit, and every future run should check it explicitly alongside the other four, especially given the probe is unresolved heading into NVDA's own 11/17 print (6 days out) and today's separate, related 11/18-briefing RSVP deadline. **Process note for the next weekly distillation:** flag to Jash that the analyst team's framing of this story has been incomplete for multiple consecutive reports (the congressional-briefing angle without the board-investigation angle) — worth a direct ask to the analyst personas to re-verify against primary board/company statements (not just aggregator sites) given how much more consequential the full story is than the partial one they'd been carrying.

**⚠️ ESCALATION, 2026-11-12 ~08:4x ET — closest the governance disqualifier has come to firing, still NOT fired.** NVDA opened pre-market down ~8-9% (live Robinhood: $158.38, tight bid/ask $158.35/$158.38, vs. 11/11's official close $173.73) on fresh reporting (CNBC "Nvidia stock falls 8% as investors await Jensen Huang's fate," NBC News, Yahoo Finance live-blog, Benzinga, all dated/titled 11/12) that **the board met Wednesday evening (11/11) and WSJ reported it is weighing whether to force Huang out**, a materially sharper framing than the 11/8 "independent investigation, no finding yet" story this plan's addendum above already priced in. **Countervailing fact, found the same run:** the board's own public statement (still the only on-record company/board position, dated 11/8-11/9, re-confirmed by a dedicated fresh search this run) says it has "no reason to believe" secrets or national security were compromised, expresses confidence in Huang's leadership, and states he remains CEO "without interruption" during the probe; President Trump, asked directly whether Huang should stay on, said "I think so." **No source found this run confirms an actual decision, resignation, or removal — this is reported board deliberation/market speculation ("awaiting Huang's fate"), not a consummated board action.** Per the plan's own §(addendum) three-leg disqualifier, this means **disqualifier (f) ("Huang's resignation, removal, or any board action limiting his authority") has NOT fired** — a report that the board is *discussing* removal is not itself "a board action limiting his authority." Buffer to the $180 revisit line is now **-12.0%** ($158.38), by a wide margin the deepest breach on record (prior worst: -7.38%, 11/10). **Decision: no trade.** Selling into an already-8-9%-priced-in, unconfirmed-outcome headline would be exactly the late reactive panic rules 1/2/3 exist to prevent; buying the dip is equally premature (no fresh MS/BW cross-vet of this specific escalation exists yet — all five analyst reports on file predate this morning's news by 12-20+ hours). **Explicit instruction for the next run and the analyst team: this is now the single highest-priority item to re-check every run until resolved.** If a credible report confirms an actual board decision (Huang resigns, is removed, or any formal board action curtailing his authority), that fires disqualifier (f) for real and mandates the full 5-analyst re-read the plan already specifies — treat that as a same-day, not same-minute (rule 3), decision point. Until then, continuation of an unresolved, deepening headline-driven drawdown is not itself a new trigger. Given the account is unattended and this is the book's single largest position cratering on CEO-ouster speculation 5 days before its own earnings print, this finding was also escalated to Jash directly via push notification this run, outside the normal weekly-summary cadence.

**✅ DISQUALIFIER (f) FIRED FOR REAL, 2026-11-12 ~09:4x ET — the first time any disqualifier/structural trigger in this book's history has actually fired, not merely approached.** This run's own fresh WebSearch (query: "Nvidia Jensen Huang resigns CEO Colette Kress interim November 12 2026", extended mode) independently confirms, across at least seven distinct outlets dated today (Investing.com, PCGamer, Cointelegraph, Yahoo Finance live-blog, mixed-news.com, mlq.ai, and Parade citing the Wall Street Journal) — not a single-source leak — that **Nvidia's board has announced Jensen Huang's resignation as President, CEO, and board member, effective immediately; CFO Colette Kress (CFO since 2013) is named interim CEO while the board runs an internal/external search for a permanent successor; Harvey C. Jones was named the board's first-ever independent chair.** This is the same finding MS's own 09:17 ET report surfaced first via its own WebSearch — this run's search independently re-derives it from different source outlets, satisfying rule 4's "don't trust a single source" bar with a second, independent confirmation. **Per the plan's own §(escalation) wording, this is a consummated board action, not discussion/speculation — disqualifier (f) ("Huang's resignation, removal, or any board action limiting his authority") has fired for real.** Live Robinhood price this run (09:38 ET, regular session, tight spread $158.67/$158.71): NVDA last trade **$158.74**, vs. 11/11's official close $173.73 (**-8.63%**) — one source (Yahoo Finance) reports the stock "sank 13% at the open," so today's regular-session price reflects a partial recovery off the opening low, not the full initial shock. Buffer to the $180 contingency-plan-v2 line: **-11.81%**, essentially matching this morning's pre-market -12.01% reading, not a fresh deepening. **Decision on what this means for the position, weighing all five analyst inputs:** MS's own fresh read (09:17 ET, the only analyst report that had caught this before this run) explicitly does **not** call this a sell — Nvidia's moat/backlog/bench are intact and Kress is a credible 13-year insider, not an unknown placeholder — but retires the stale 9/23 $192.0 base-case DCF as unreliable through a confirmed leadership change, substituting a first-pass governance-stress estimate (~$112-125/sh under a WACC stressed to 13.0-14.0%) that would put NVDA **between modestly overvalued and roughly fairly valued** at current prices — a sharp reversal from the prior clean-undervaluation read. JPM (08:18 ET, predates the resignation) still frames this as "hold through the print, no pre-print action," now complicated by the fact that Kress, not Huang, will run the 11/17 call for the first time in the company's history — a genuinely new variable JPM's own brief hasn't yet addressed. **Critically, BW's risk read (11/11 ~13:41 ET) and BR's allocation read (11/11 ~15:13 ET) both entirely predate this event** — both were still working from the "unresolved investigation, Huang stays CEO" framing, and neither has assessed concentration or sizing against an actually-confirmed leadership change. **Per this book's own established cross-vetting discipline (rule 6, applied identically to every prior structural-break review on OMCL/GEHC's revisit lines — MS's valuation view alone has never been sufficient to act on, fresh BW risk + BR allocation input is required before any sizing/trim decision), that bar is not met today.** Weighed against rules 1-3 (no same-minute decisions, don't chase a move already 8%+ priced in, a structural break mandates a re-read not an automatic reaction): **selling into an already-sharply-down, still-settling move before BW/BR have even seen the confirmed news would itself be the reactive mistake this framework exists to prevent — but so would treating "MS says not a sell" as license to do nothing further.** **Decision: NO TRADE this run.** This fire is logged as the live, top-priority decision point for every run until resolved — not closed, not deferred to the weekly summary. **Explicit instruction for the next run and the analyst team: BW and BR must both post fresh reads reflecting the confirmed resignation (not the prior "investigation unresolved" framing) before this desk can responsibly reach a cross-vetted trim/hold conclusion — treat obtaining those two reads as the single highest-priority task, and re-evaluate the position the moment they're on file, same-day per rule 3, without waiting for a further news development.** Checked history per step 3 (`git log --oneline -- trading-experiment/state.md`): every one of this book's ~100+ prior "line breached, no structural fact" entries this week resolved to hold because the underlying fact pattern was unresolved speculation, continuation, or a price level without a dated catalyst — this is structurally different, the first time the actual named disqualifying event has happened, which is exactly why the response is a demand for fresh cross-desk input rather than another "no new fact" hold. Given the magnitude (largest position, confirmed CEO departure after 33 years, interim CFO-CEO, 5 days before its own earnings print, account unattended between scheduled runs), this finding was escalated to Jash directly via push notification this run.

**✅ PRINT HAPPENED, STRUCTURAL CHECKLIST CLEAN, PLAN'S PRINT-SPECIFIC SCOPE RESOLVED — 2026-11-18 ~08:38 ET, the mandatory first-post-print re-read per §3.** NVDA reported Q3 FY2027 last night (11/17 AMC): **revenue $115.8B** (vs. the $108.0B±2% guide and ~$108.8-109.3B consensus — a beat of ~6-7% above the top of guidance), **Data Center $107.6B, +110% YoY** (accelerating, not decelerating), **non-GAAP EPS $2.68** (vs. ~$2.47-2.49 consensus), GAAP/non-GAAP gross margin 74.8%/74.9%. **Q4 FY27 guide: $130B±3%**, well above the ~$125.3B Street estimate, still assuming zero China Data Center compute revenue (no change to that assumption, no incremental deterioration). Vera Rubin NVL72 has shipped; Kress (first call as CEO) called it "off to an explosive start." Board Chairman Harvey Jones explicitly confirmed Huang will **not** be reinstated — a clarification of the already-known 11/12 resignation, not a new disqualifying event. Checked each of §2's four structural disqualifiers fresh against the actual print (independently corroborated via this run's own extended WebSearch — SEC 8-K/press release, Benzinga, Qz — not just JPM's brief): **(1) Data Center deceleration below ~100%+ YoY — NOT MET, accelerated to +110%. (2) Guide undershoot of the $108.0B±2% band — NOT MET, beat decisively. (3) Negative incremental China/export development — NOT MET, zero-China assumption unchanged, nothing new found. (4) A credible sourced Blackwell/GB300 supply/demand problem — NOT MET, nothing found; the opposite (explosive Vera Rubin demand, hyperscaler POs in hand per GS's 11/17 report).** **Zero of the four structural disqualifiers fired — this was a clean, strong beat on every dimension, not a dip and not a miss.** Market reaction was the third "beat-and-fade" pattern in four quarters (closed $155.46 -0.44% pre-print, popped to ~$162.94 post-print, faded overnight to ~$157-158, now ~$160.11 pre-market this run, +3.0% vs. yesterday's close) — per §1, a muted/fading reaction to a clean beat is explicitly not itself a trim signal, and per §4 this isn't even a "dip" to potentially add into. **Decision: HOLD, no trim, no add on NVDA.** This also independently satisfies rule 6's cross-vetting bar for *not* acting: **none of MS, BW, GS, or BR has posted a read since the print** (MS 09:16 ET 11/17, BW 13:41 ET 11/17, GS 14:4x ET 11/17, BR 15:12 ET 11/17 — all four pre-print) — only JPM (08:17 ET today) has, giving the post-print recap this entry draws on. Mirroring the 11/12 Huang-resignation precedent (demand fresh cross-desk input before any sizing call, don't let one desk's read stand in for the team's), **the single highest-priority task for the next run and the analyst team: obtain MS's full ground-up NVDA rebuild (promised for "the first run after the print" in its 11/17 report), plus fresh BW risk and BR allocation reads reflecting the actual beat** — not to authorize an add (rule 20's no-new-cash-to-NVDA instruction and rule 6a's pause both still independently block that regardless of how the rebuild reads), but because a position this size deserves an updated fair-value and risk picture against real numbers rather than stale pre-print ones. **Per §7, this plan's scope was the 2026-11-17 print specifically; that event has now occurred and resolved cleanly (no disqualifier fired, hold). The print-specific decision point is CLOSED.** The governance/leadership-continuity disqualifier (f) that fired 11/12 remains a standing fact (Huang will not return, Kress remains interim CEO, succession search ongoing, the board investigation and the newly-GS-surfaced Kistenmacher securities class action both still open) — that overhang does not "close" with the print, it simply didn't deepen tonight, and MS's own post-print rebuild is squarely the place to re-price it, not this contingency plan. A fresh print-specific plan should be written closer to NVDA's ~February 2027 print, per rule 16, rather than assuming this one carries forward.

**⚠️ SUPERSEDED — REINSTATEMENT, 2026-11-23 ~09:37 ET.** The line above ("Huang will not be reinstated," 11/18) is now overtaken by events: NVIDIA's own board, per its own investor-relations press release dated today and independently corroborated by CBS News, NBC News, AP, and Yahoo Finance, voted **unanimously to reinstate Jensen Huang as President, CEO, and board director, effective immediately** — Colette Kress returns to her prior CFO role (having served as interim CEO since 11/12), Harvey Jones remains board chair, and the board's independent investigation into the foreign-intelligence-targeting allegations continues. This is the mirror image of disqualifier (f) (which fired 11/12 on the resignation) — a scenario this plan never contemplated (reversal, not deepening/confirmation of the governance break). NVDA gapped +29.3% vs. Friday's close on the news (confirmed live via Robinhood). **Treated as a major new structural fact, not a same-minute trading signal**: rule 2 (don't chase a +29% single-session move) and rule 6 (no fresh BW/BR read yet reflecting the confirmed reinstatement — MS's 09:13 ET report predates full confirmation and still treated it as contested) both independently counsel no action today regardless of how bullish the fundamental read eventually comes in; rule 20/6a's no-new-cash-to-NVDA instruction and the rate-shock pause also still apply.
